14:T5e3,## Problem

`composeState` serializes embedding-gated providers ahead of every Stage-1 call. On a direct-Cerebras deployment (fast models, telemetry below), the context-assembly floor is **0.7–3.4s per turn before the routing model even starts**:

```
 total  ttreply  stage1  embed  compose  relconv  chatctx
  3351     3156    1176   1984     1600     1598      794
  6042     5853    1650   1012     2329     1963     1442
  7375     7212    1666   1447     2445     2131     1345
  4478     4232    1804   2559     1834     1833      538
  6322     6047    2010   1579     2248     1789     2243
```
(`InferenceTiming` spans; median composeState 1.2s across 20 live turns; the two ~15ms rows are reused-state turns, showing the ceiling when assembly is skipped.)

`model:TEXT_EMBEDDING` runs ×2 (query embedding for `relevant-conversations` + one more) and `PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` adds 0.3–3.4s. On a 1.2s-stage-1 backend this floor is 40–60% of time-to-reply.

## Ask

Make the embedding-gated providers non-blocking or degrading: kick off `relevant-conversations` (and its embeddings) in parallel with the fast providers and give it a soft deadline — when it misses, compose without it rather than serializing every turn behind it. `PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` deserves the same soft-deadline treatment.

Telemetry: captured on a live Discord agent, Cerebras direct (gemma-4-31b small / zai-glm-4.7 large), develop @ 3e47d12ba03.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
15:T47c,## Problem

Once routing is fast, **output tokens are the remaining time-to-reply driver** on chat turns (~3ms/token at the provider): a paragraph-length reply costs 3-6s regardless of how fast the runtime is. Live examples (Cerebras direct, glm-4.7): short replies 1.9-2.4s total; explanation-length replies 4.5-7.4s total with Stage-1 model spans of 4.2-6.8s that are almost entirely visible output generation.

Today the only brevity control is prose in `character.system`, which models honor inconsistently and which can't vary by surface (a Discord group reply wants 1-2 sentences; a DM or docs question can run longer).

## Ask

A structural reply-length budget: per-agent (and ideally per-channel-type) `maxReplyTokens`-style knob that flows into the Stage-1/synthesis calls as a real `max_tokens`-bounded budget plus a rendered length instruction — so operators can pin group-chat turns terse without rewriting personas, and the budget is enforced by the provider rather than requested politely.

Captured on a live Discord agent, Cerebras direct, develop @ 3e47d12ba03.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
16:T55f,## Problem

On Cerebras reasoning models (zai-glm-4.7, gpt-oss-120b), hidden reasoning tokens dominate tail latency — and the `InferenceTiming` telemetry can't see them. Live Stage-1 latencies on the SAME model/prompt-shape ranged **1.2s → 12.8s**:

```
 stage1(ms):  1176  1000  1650  1666   821  12791  6790  4689  2010  1804  10005  1293  4222
```

Direct-API measurement of the discrepancy (same prompt, zai-glm-4.7):
- default: **813ms, 400 reasoning tokens** (of 407 completion tokens)
- `reasoning_effort: "none"`: **158ms, 0 reasoning tokens**

#16299 wires thinking-off suppression for calls that carry `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"`, which covers Stage-1 and the evaluator — but the variance above shows bursts still landing in production, and without reasoning-token counts in the telemetry we can't tell suppressed-but-queued apart from unsuppressed.

## Ask

1. Surface `completion_tokens_details.reasoning_tokens` (Cerebras/OpenAI both return it) in `InferenceTiming` spans so reasoning bursts are attributable.
2. Audit which model calls in the turn pipeline do NOT carry `thinking: "off"` (planner iterations, callback rewrites, failure-reply helpers) and bound them on reasoning models the same way.

Captured on a live Discord agent, Cerebras direct, develop @ 3e47d12ba03.

18:T8c7,## Summary

With `LOG_FILE=true`, the logger opens `output.log`, `prompts.log`, and `chat.log`, but `output.log` stays at 0 bytes forever. `prompts.log`/`chat.log` (via `logPrompt`/`logChatIn` etc.) work fine.

## Root cause

`stripAnsi` in `packages/logger/src/logger.ts` (line 376 on current `develop`) builds an invalid regex — the last alternative has one escape level too many:

```ts
const ESC = "\x1b";
const BEL = "\x07";
// throws SyntaxError: Invalid regular expression: Unterminated group
new RegExp(
  `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\\\(B))`,
  "g",
);
```

`\\\\(B` cooks to the regex source `\\(B` — an escaped *backslash* followed by an **unclosed group** `(B` — so the outer `(?:` never closes and `new RegExp` throws on **every** call. The intended pattern is clearly `\(B` (the `ESC ( B` charset-designation sequence), i.e. one escape level less.

`writeLogEntryToFile` is the only caller of `stripAnsi`, and its bare `catch {}` swallows the `SyntaxError`, so every entry write dies silently: the console/Adze path and the in-memory ring buffer still work, the three files still get opened by `ensureFileLog`, and `output.log` never receives a byte. As far as I can tell this has never worked. The prompt/chat writers don't call `stripAnsi`, which is why they're unaffected.

## Repro

1. `LOG_FILE=true` + run any agent → `output.log` is created but stays 0 bytes while the console logs normally.
2. Or just paste the `new RegExp` snippet above into `bun repl` / `node` — it throws.

## Fix

```diff
     const re = new RegExp(
-      `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\\\(B))`,
+      `${ESC}(?:\\[[\\x20-\\x3F]*[\\x40-\\x7E]|\\].*?(?:${BEL}|${ESC}\\\\|\\(B))`,
       "g",
     );
```

Verified locally by applying this to the built bundle: the regex compiles, SGR sequences are stripped correctly (`"\x1b[36mInfo\x1b[39m hi"` → `"Info hi"`), and `output.log` immediately starts receiving timestamped entries.

Worth considering separately: `writeLogEntryToFile`'s silent `catch {}` hid a 100%-reproducible failure for the sink's entire lifetime — a one-time stderr warning on the first swallowed error would have surfaced this instantly.
19:T840,## What

The shared vitest base config excludes two agent files from coverage reporting entirely:

```
packages/test/vitest/default.config.ts
  coverage.exclude:
    // Rolldown coverage still struggles with these inline type-import files.
    "eliza/packages/agent/src/api/server.ts",
    "eliza/packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts",
```

The enforced changed-file coverage gate (`.github/workflows/coverage-gate.yml` → `scripts/security/coverage-gate.awk`) fails any changed source file that is **missing from the merged LCOV**:

```
for (f in changed_map) {
  if (!(f in file_pct)) { printf "  MISSING: %s\n", f; missing_count++ }
```

So every PR that touches `packages/agent/src/runtime/eliza.ts` or `packages/agent/src/api/server.ts` fails the gate as `MISSING` — even with a changed test that executes the changed lines — because the config forbids those files from ever appearing in a coverage report. The two escapes cancel each other: the gate demands the file in LCOV, the config guarantees it never is.

(Note the exclude paths also carry a stale `eliza/` prefix from the old wrapper-repo layout, so they may not even match under the current roots — in which case the *comment's* Rolldown problem is unhandled instead, and the file's absence in practice comes from coverage collection failing on it. Either way the outcome for a PR author is the same: structural `MISSING`.)

## Live example

#16402 is a one-line boot-seeding fix in `eliza.ts` with a changed unit test (`packages/agent/src/runtime/__tests__/model-env-seed.test.ts`) that imports the module and drives `configureLocalEmbeddingPlugin` for real — and the file still cannot appear in LCOV.

## Options

1. Fix the underlying coverage-collection failure on those files and drop the excludes (best).
2. Teach the gate an explicit, visible allowlist for config-excluded files (fail-open with a printed warning instead of a hard `MISSING`).
3. At minimum, update the exclude paths to the current layout and document the trap where agent contributors will see it.

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
1a:T714,## Problem

PGlite is instantiated **in-process on the main thread** (`plugins/plugin-sql/src/pglite/manager.ts` — plain `new PGlite`, no worker). Every WASM query execution blocks the event loop, which (a) serializes what look like concurrent provider awaits in `composeState` and (b) inflates every in-flight span — during the #16393 investigation, provider embedding calls measuring 230–320ms raw showed as 500–2500ms because their continuations couldn't run while WASM ground through queries.

#16405 removes the worst single query (unindexed vector full-scan → HNSW/KNN), but *all* PGlite work still executes on the loop: memory reads, entity resolution, the per-turn 2000-row hash-memory BM25 scan in relevant-conversations, trajectory writes.

## Ask

Host PGlite in a `worker_threads` worker with an RPC seam, so WASM execution never blocks the runtime loop. Notes from a feasibility pass:
- `@electric-sql/pglite` (0.4.6 vendored) ships a `worker` entry (`PGliteWorker` + `worker()` wrapper) — designed for web workers, but the leader/proxy protocol is transport-agnostic; a Node `worker_threads` adapter around the same message protocol is the likely shape.
- Insertion point: `plugins/plugin-sql/src/pglite/manager.ts` `createClient` (~:305–333). The drizzle pglite driver needs a client honoring the PGlite interface; `PGliteWorker` implements it.
- Care points: extension loading (`vector`, `fuzzystrmatch`) must happen worker-side; live-query/notification APIs if used; clean shutdown ordering (the manager already serializes close); benchmark a representative turn before/after.

This is the remaining structural half of the #16393 latency floor (the discarded-refetch half shipped in #16403, index half in #16405).

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)
1b:T8d5,## Problem

The root `test:cloud` command is not self-contained after a deterministic clean install when artifact synchronization is disabled. It begins the Cloud test batches without first making workspace runtime exports such as `@elizaos/core` available.

Other top-level lanes (`test:plugins`, `test:client`, and `test:server`) explicitly run `build:core`; `test:cloud` directly invokes `packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs`.

## Reproduction

On current `develop` in a new worktree:

```bash
ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile
node packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs
bun run test:cloud
```

The first database/service batches fail with repeated errors shaped like:

```text
Cannot find module '@elizaos/core' from 'plugins/plugin-sql/src/index.ts'
```

This cascades into PGlite readiness failures even though the tested Cloud code is healthy.

Then:

```bash
bun run build:core
bun run test:cloud
```

The standard build completes 70/70 Turbo tasks and the same Cloud runner completes all 10 batches with zero failed tests.

## Why this matters

- A documented root test script should not depend on a previously downloaded/built artifact bundle that is absent from a clean checkout.
- `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1` is used to prevent stale artifact bundles from overwriting checked-in fixtures/baselines during deterministic validation.
- The current failure shape looks like dozens of database regressions rather than one missing prerequisite.
- PGlite tests correctly fail loudly, so the harness should satisfy their runtime dependency rather than weakening those guards.

## Acceptance criteria

- [ ] `bun run test:cloud` succeeds from a clean frozen install with `ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1`.
- [ ] Cloud tests resolve required workspace packages without relying on a downloaded prebuilt artifact bundle.
- [ ] Use the smallest standard prerequisite or test-runtime mechanism; do not require an unnecessary full-repo build if a narrower supported path exists.
- [ ] Preserve PGlite readiness assertions and fail-closed behavior.
- [ ] Add a CI/self-test covering the clean-install, no-artifact-sync path.
- [ ] Document any required keyword generation/build step in the command itself rather than as tribal knowledge.
1c:T499,## Problem

`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness/src/cli-run.test.ts` assigns a temporary `HOME`, but the CLI derives evidence and secret paths from `node:os.homedir()`. Under Bun, `homedir()` remained the real account home, so a test run created evidence below `/home/nubs/.moltbot/projects/eliza-fleet/evidence/voice-e2e`.

This contaminates developer or CI state and risks coupling a test to real local credentials. The two observed temporary evidence directories were removed after diagnosis.

## Acceptance criteria

- Add an explicit injectable evidence root and a test-safe secret or homedir seam.
- Preserve the current live default under the real user home.
- Assert every generated artifact remains below the test `mkdtemp` root on success and failure.
- Assert the real `~/.moltbot` tree has no delta after the test.
- Exercise the contract under both Node and Bun.
- Keep live-provider behavior and evidence naming unchanged outside tests.

## Evidence

The leak reproduced while running the baseline CLI evidence test during #16173 follow-up validation. Setting `process.env.HOME` was insufficient under Bun because `os.homedir()` still resolved to `/home/nubs`.1d:T725,## Problem

The `develop` push for merge commit `6ef46e6bc977facb0a3ac656dc521f4f76bd1f31` exposed a repository formatting failure in `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/__tests__/unit/text-user-reasoning-effort.test.ts`.

`Format + Type Safety Ratchet` run [29285095066](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29285095066), job [86935668803](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29285095066/job/86935668803), reports that Biome would reflow the `it.each` model-name array at line 70. The file is byte-identical on that merge's base and result (`017992dbbcbdaf79acfcea135e8299a5c3f8f14917da69bda27a108f74eda9aa`), so this is a pre-existing defect rather than a change from the merged PR.

A read-only repository-wide `format:check --continue=always` then identified exactly three additional pre-existing Biome defects. All four affected files are unchanged from the restoration PR's base through current `develop`:

- `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/__tests__/unit/text-user-reasoning-effort.test.ts`
- `plugins/plugin-commands/__tests__/model-config-command.test.ts`
- `plugins/plugin-commands/src/actions/model-config.ts`
- `packages/core/src/runtime/shortcut-registry.test.ts`

## Scope

- Apply only the repository formatter's canonical reflows to those four files.

Related exact-head hold: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16293#issuecomment-49624929741f:T939,## Problem

`zai-glm-4.7` — the default `TEXT_LARGE` model on Cerebras since #16261 — is a hybrid reasoning model (streams a separate `reasoning` delta channel), but `isReasoningModel()` in `plugins/plugin-openai/models/text.ts` does not match it. Two consequences on a local Cerebras runtime (`ELIZA_PROVIDER=cerebras`, `OPENAI_LARGE_MODEL=zai-glm-4.7`):

1. The Cerebras-mode `reasoning_effort: "low"` default (added for exactly this failure on `gpt-oss-120b`) never applies to GLM, so its reasoning runs unbounded.
2. `providerOptions.eliza.thinking = "off"` — set by core's planner loop (`packages/core/src/runtime/planner-loop.ts:1549`), Stage-1 formatting calls (`packages/core/src/services/message.ts:8770`), and the app-model-tester — is silently ignored by plugin-openai, while plugin-elizacloud maps it to each Cerebras model's suppression value (`resolveCerebrasThinkingOffReasoningEffort`, `plugins/plugin-elizacloud/src/models/text.ts:366`).

With a capped `max_tokens` the model spends the entire budget on hidden reasoning and returns **empty visible content** with `finish_reason: "length"`.

## Reproduction (live Cerebras, wire-captured via a logging proxy)

`POST /api/model-tester/run {"test":"text-large"}` on a default local Cerebras setup, 8 runs. Every request went out as `{"model":"zai-glm-4.7","max_tokens":160}` with **no** `reasoning_effort`:

```
4x  finish_reason:"length"  reasoning_tokens:160/160  → "TEXT_LARGE returned empty text"
4x  finish_reason:"stop"    reasoning_tokens:62–157   → "pong"
```

~50% failure, purely dependent on how long GLM happens to reason. Direct curl confirms the mechanism: `reasoning_effort:"low"` bounds reasoning (~20–30 tokens), and `reasoning_effort:"none"` (accepted by gemma/GLM, rejected by gpt-oss) eliminates it entirely (`reasoning_tokens: 0`).

## Fix

PR incoming, mirroring plugin-elizacloud's settled semantics:

- add the GLM family to `isReasoningModel()` so the Cerebras `"low"` default applies;
- honor `eliza.thinking === "off"` in Cerebras mode by mapping it per model: `"none"` for gemma/GLM, `"low"` for gpt-oss; explicit caller `providerOptions.openai.reasoningEffort` still wins.

After the fix (same wire capture): 10/10 probes return content, `finish_reason:"stop"`, `reasoning_tokens: 0`.

## Risk

## Scope

Package-dist assembly permits non-canonical local state to affect published output in two ways:

1. `prepare-package-dist.mjs` recursively discovers manifests. A tracked nested manifest such as `plugins/plugin-sql/src/package.json` can repeat a canonical workspace name with a different version and impersonate the workspace root during dependency rewriting.
2. `copy-package-assets.mjs` can copy generated Python cache, coverage, virtual-environment, and package-build residue into publish directories.

Resolve dependency versions through the repository's shared canonical workspace-discovery seam, reject duplicate canonical workspace identities, and exclude generic Python-generated residue while retaining source assets. This scope does not add generated packaging-runtime output rules or change application/runtime behavior.

## Acceptance Criteria

- [ ] A nested manifest repeating a canonical workspace name cannot override the canonical workspace version.
- [ ] Duplicate names among canonical workspace roots fail with the conflicting identity and paths.
- [ ] Root workspace inclusions and negations remain the only source of workspace membership.
- [ ] Malformed selected workspace manifests fail rather than disappearing from dependency rewriting.
- [ ] `__pycache__`, `.pyc`/`.pyo`, Python test/type/lint caches, virtual environments, coverage residue, and `*.egg-info` directories are not copied.
- [ ] Ordinary Python source remains present.
- [ ] Focused tests cover the nested collision, duplicate and malformed canonical manifests, workspace negation, cache exclusions, and retained source.
- [ ] Representative agent, app-core, and UI builds and package dry-runs succeed.
- [ ] No package manifests, lockfiles, or workflows change.

## Evidence Plan

- [ ] Screenshots or recording: N/A - no rendered UI source or user flow changes.
- [ ] Logs: focused tests, shared workspace-seam tests, representative builds, and package dry-runs.
- [ ] Real-LLM trajectories: N/A - no model, prompt, provider, action, or agent-loop behavior changes.

## Exact evidence

Run: https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29171752918
Artifact: chat-latency-paired-staging-5710071096eff7a62c13ec1f7d9bd3fdfa10a110

| Case | Placement | Gateway headers | Route total | Auth | Mid | Reserve |

## Current path

The chat handler first calls resolveInferenceAuthContext. For an API key it:

1. reads the inference auth context from the shared cache;
2. on a hit, returns the fully authorized user/org/key context;
3. on a miss, runs requireAuthOrApiKeyWithOrg;
4. checks moderation authoritatively; and
5. writes the positive context back to cache.

Worker auto-cache configuration prefers the bound CACHE_KV namespace. Therefore the current evidence proves a cache-hit versus authoritative-path split, but does not yet identify whether the 3.2 seconds are KV read/miss behavior, database auth/org queries, moderation, the cache write, or cold dependency/connection setup. #16079 must name those boundaries before optimizing.

Relevant code:
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-context.ts
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-cache.ts
- packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/cache/client.ts
- packages/cloud/api/v1/chat/completions/route.ts

## Required work

- Add privacy-safe correlated timings for credential extraction, cache availability, KV hit/miss/invalid, authoritative key lookup, user/org lookup, moderation, and cache write.
- Record only bounded source/result enums and timings; never log presented keys, hashes, auth headers, prompts, or user-controlled identifiers.
- Reproduce cache hits and controlled cache misses in staging across fresh Worker versions/placements.
- Optimize the proven slow boundary. Candidates include a single authoritative join, parallel independent checks, Hyperdrive/connection placement, or a strongly consistent edge auth primitive. Choose from evidence.
- Preserve fail-closed invalid/revoked/inactive/suspended behavior and exact 401/403 taxonomy. Do not add an un-invalidated positive L1 cache or relax revocation to reduce latency.

2a:T1152,## Summary

Every Eliza Cloud email magic link should also contain a six-digit, single-use companion code. A user on a constrained or cross-device client such as LP3 can type the code on the existing login page while the magic link remains available and unchanged.

This is blocked on an additive Steward auth contract. Eliza is only the Steward client/proxy and does not own challenge storage or email rendering.

Signed: **[sol-orch]**

## Recon findings

- Eliza login calls `@stwd/sdk` `StewardAuth.signInWithEmail()` from `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/login/steward-login-section.tsx`.
- `/steward/*` is a signed upstream proxy in `packages/cloud/api/src/steward/embedded.ts`.
- The link callback is `packages/ui/src/cloud/public-pages/pages/auth/email-callback-page.tsx`, which calls `verifyEmailCallback()` and syncs the Steward session cookie.
- Steward owns `POST /auth/email/send`, `POST /auth/email/verify`, token persistence, tenant email templates, and the mail provider.
- Steward already has six-digit email OTP endpoints, but they intentionally return a short-lived `emailGrant` for passkey registration. They do not log the user in.
- Steward also has device-code approval/poll primitives, but those are a separate protocol and are not aliases for an email magic-link challenge.

Calling both existing send methods is not acceptable. It produces independent challenges, may produce two messages, cannot invalidate both credentials when either succeeds, and would broaden the passkey grant's security contract.

## Required upstream dependency

Add an additive Steward combined challenge:

1. `POST /auth/email/send` creates one challenge and sends one email containing the existing link plus a six-digit code.
2. Link-token hash and code verifier resolve to one central, purpose-bound challenge record.
3. The central record is atomically consumed, so a successful link invalidates the code and a successful code invalidates the link, including concurrent attempts.
4. Add `POST /auth/email/code/verify`, then expose an SDK method returning the normal `StewardAuthResult | StewardMfaRequiredResult`.
5. Leave `sendEmailOtp`, `verifyEmailOtp`, and `emailGrant` behavior unchanged.

Security requirements:

- server-side cryptographic random generation,
- short shared TTL, recommended 10 minutes,
- hash/keyed verifier at rest, no plaintext token/code,
- bound to normalized email, tenant, and `email-login` purpose,
- atomic single use and cross-invalidation,
- per-IP plus per-challenge/target rate limits,
- maximum five failed attempts and hard lock,
- constant-time verifier compare where applicable,
- no code, email, raw token, or full challenge alias in logs,
- no account-enumeration differences,
- normal tenant, SSO, allowed-method, and MFA gates.

## Eliza acceptance criteria after Steward SDK release

- Existing link callback remains unchanged and valid.
- The `email-sent` state includes optional copy: `Enter the 6-digit code from your email`.
- Accessible input uses `inputMode="numeric"`, `autoComplete="one-time-code"`, numeric paste sanitization, `maxLength={6}`, and an explicit label.
- Code success uses the existing `handleSuccess()` session/cookie flow.
- Explicit loading, invalid, expired, locked/too-many-attempts, and transport-error states.
- Resend has a visible cooldown and requests a fresh combined challenge.
- Existing `packages/ui` tokens and Lucide icons only, with LP3 touch sizing.
- Focused backend, SDK, email-template/provider, UI, callback regression, and LP3 viewport tests.

## Collision check

- #16368 and #16365 concern account-provider reauthentication and are separate.
- #15483 covered LP3 WebView/handoff/CORS failures and is closed. This feature only overlaps at the constrained-device UX boundary and must not absorb those bugs.
- No matching open Eliza PR or issue was found on 2026-07-14.

## Evidence

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Full Eliza initiation, proxy, callback, and session path traced | PASS |
| Steward storage, template/provider, OTP, and device-code primitives traced | PASS |
| Safe one-PR implementation available in Eliza | NO, blocked on Steward API/SDK |
| Eliza code changed | NO, intentionally |
| Prod/secrets changed | NO |

A detailed executable dossier is available locally at `projects/eliza-fleet/CLOUD-MAGIC-LINK-OTP-2026-07-14.md`.

# Risks

Medium. This changes when a successful inference-auth cache population write completes on Cloudflare Workers. The API key, user/organization, and moderation checks still finish authoritatively before authorization returns; invalid, revoked/inactive, suspended, unavailable-cache, and malformed-input paths remain fail-closed. Worker requests register the positive write with `executionCtx.waitUntil`; non-Worker/test callers continue to await it inline. Cache invalidation coverage protects lifecycle transitions.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Adds bounded, privacy-safe, trace-correlated timings for credential extraction, cache availability/read/write, API-key lookup, user/org lookup, moderation, and total auth resolution.
- Makes inference-auth cache availability and outcomes explicit instead of conflating unavailable/malformed data with a healthy miss.
- Uses a focused inference API-key resolver that preserves the existing errors, validation order, and usage accounting while allowing controlled probes and cache-failure recovery to measure authoritative repositories.
- Defers only the post-authorization positive KV population write to the Worker lifetime, with a second correlated completion record for the actual backend outcome and duration.
- Invalidates positive auth entries across API-key lifecycle and moderation changes.
- Adds an exact-version staging probe and GitHub workflow that deploys a uniquely named workers.dev-only diagnostic Worker, creates an isolated auth fixture, captures 30 retained hits + 10 controlled misses + fail-closed guards, sanitizes Tail logs, enforces thresholds, and always removes the Worker and fixture.
- Proves Worker Tail readiness with a real authenticated trace before retaining samples. The readiness boundary retries only the observed transient workers.dev propagation 404, fails immediately on other HTTP statuses, and emits only sanitized Wrangler stderr on exhaustion.
- Adds a narrow Gitleaks exception for the deterministic public `unit-probe-token:<32 lowercase hex>` test fixture in its single test file; the rest of that file and the full branch range remain scanned.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix and performance improvement for the cloud inference-auth hot path, plus diagnostic/test infrastructure.

# Documentation changes needed?

No public documentation change is needed. The new workflow and probe are self-describing operational diagnostics, and the runtime contract remains unchanged.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. Open the [successful exact-SHA staging run](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29322590272).
2. Download the [sanitized report and correlated Tail logs](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29322590272/artifacts/8306610038), artifact `inference-auth-staging-460efacc78d640c1269234a17f4ab25c32f431bd`.
3. Inspect `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-auth-context.ts`, then `packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/inference-api-key-auth.ts`, `packages/scripts/cloud/inference-auth-latency.mjs`, and `.github/workflows/chat-latency-live.yml`.

## Detailed testing steps

The final exact-version run produced:

| Auth interval | n | p50 | p90 | p95 | max |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| Exact-version cache hit | 30 | 2 ms | 3 ms | 3 ms | 6 ms |
| Controlled cache miss | 10 | 271 ms | 281 ms | 406 ms | 406 ms |

The controlled-miss p90 is **91.2% lower** than the issue's 3,204 ms evidence, with no multi-second auth tail. Controlled-miss stage p90s: key 57 ms, user/org 102 ms, moderation 53 ms, KV read 73 ms. Deferred KV writes completed at p90 254 ms; 10/10 were observed as `written`. The single 890 ms deferred-write maximum remained outside the response path.

Worker Tail readiness required six authenticated probes before capture. Only after that trace appeared did the workflow retain the 43 measured requests, eliminating the process-live/websocket-not-ready race that an earlier diagnostic run exposed.

Fail-closed guards in the same run:

- Invalid key: HTTP 401, authoritative `rejected`, write `not_run`.
- Suspended key: HTTP 403, authoritative `suspended`, write `not_run`.
- Forged controlled-probe request: HTTP 400, cached authorization with probe `off`; the request could not force an authoritative miss.

The [pre-optimization exact-version run](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29314229862) measured controlled-miss p90 679 ms and KV-write p90 277 ms, isolating the positive KV write as the dominant controllable boundary.

2e:T168f,## What & why

Next slice of the account-observability roadmap (after #16355). Users running a large credential pool get a proper **command-center table** instead of a stack of cards, so health, usage, resets, priority, and lease activity are legible at a glance and sortable.

Renders as a **desktop enhancement** (`min-width:1024px`, the same query `SettingsView` uses for the settings rail from #16354) once a provider pool has **>= 4 accounts**. Narrow/mobile widths and small pools keep the existing `AccountCard` stack (which carries the richer per-card affordances). The card view is untouched.

### Columns
account (label + email) · **health** badge (ok / needs-reauth / rate-limited / invalid, with `healthDetail` tooltip + rate-limit countdown) · session/weekly **usage** bars · **resets** (relative) · **priority** · **enabled** toggle · **last-used** (relative) · **leases** (feature-gated, see below) · **actions**.

### Row states & actions
- **Sortable** by health / usage / last-used / priority. Default surfaces worst health first (the reason to open the table). `aria-sort` lives on the header `<th>` cell.
- **needs-reauth / invalid** → a repair CTA that reuses the **existing #16314 credential-repair flow** (`replaceAccountId` path via `onReauthenticate`).
- **rate-limited** → a live "resets in …" countdown in the health cell.
- **disabled** rows are dimmed.
- Full card affordance parity preserved in the actions cell: **test**, **refresh usage**, **reorder** (priority up/down — targets the priority neighbour regardless of the current table sort), reauthenticate, and remove (confirm dialog). Every row action is an optional prop and hides when its handler is absent.
- Loading is upstream (the table only mounts with data). Empty/degenerate pool → an in-table empty row; the surface's overall empty state remains the existing add-account CTA in `AccountManagementPanel`.

### Feature-detection (does NOT depend on #16355 merging)
#16355 adds an `observability` field (`activeLeaseCount`, `lastLeaseAt`, `lastSelectedAt`, `servedLastRequest`) to each account. This PR feature-detects it: the **Leases column is hidden entirely** when no row carries observability, so it degrades gracefully on older hosts and pre-#16355 payloads — no column of lying zeros, no crash. The client types are augmented with the **optional** shape only.

### Design gate (#16367)
packages/ui tokens + lucide-react only, **no new deps**. The new files introduce **zero** `no-focus-ring-gate` / `no-widget-chrome-gate` offenders (verified against the source-scanning suites; the only account-surface offenders on `develop` are the pre-existing ones #16367 already targets, none in the new files).

30:Te0a,## Summary

Adds a burn-efficiency account-pool strategy that spends paid Anthropic capacity before each account's weekly allowance rolls over unused.

The strategy is now `drain-soonest-reset`, and is the default for `anthropic-subscription` pools. This replaces the earlier subscription-expiry-centered design after the hawktom100 incident, where an account sat at 0% weekly usage all week and then rolled into a fresh window with its paid allowance wasted.

## Design

- Primary order: relevant weekly usage window `resetsAt`, soonest first.
- Tiebreak: most remaining headroom, lowest weekly utilization first.
- Final-days booster: when reset and utilization tie, prefer the account whose subscription ends soonest within 48 hours.
- Explicit per-account priority remains a hard operator override. Generated creation-order priority does not defeat drain ordering.
- The legacy `priority` strategy remains available as a manual pool strategy.
- Anthropic's all-model `seven_day` bucket is persisted as blended `weeklyPct` / `resetsAt`.
- Anthropic `limits` entries marked `weekly_scoped` / `weekly` are persisted by model display name. A requested model such as `claude-fable-5` uses the `Fable` bucket. Unknown models fall back to blended weekly usage.
- Session exhaustion and 429 cooldown temporarily exclude an account without changing its weekly drain rank. Weekly reset timestamps are deliberately not reused as hourly/session cooldown timestamps.
- Subscription `subscriptionEndsAt` persistence and the terminal `expired` health state from the original PR remain.

## Why

Subscription end date is not the day-to-day binding resource. The scarce resource is each account's asynchronous weekly allowance, including model-scoped weekly limits. Letting a window close with unused allowance destroys paid capacity. The selector therefore burns the soonest-refunding budget first while still respecting current session caps and hand-tuned operator priority.

## Verification

Focused suites are green:

```text
core contract alignment:                                11 passed
commands account backend:                               26 passed
UI account strategy/card/eligibility:                   16 passed
TOTAL:                                                  148 passed
```

Typechecks passed for contracts, shared, and core. Biome and `git diff --check` passed.

Codex review was run before push. Findings were addressed for stale command strategy names, legacy generated-priority migration, explicit priority preservation during credential replacement, and session-cap gating before weekly drain sorting.

## Manual artifact review

I downloaded both final-run ZIPs and verified their SHA-256 values match GitHub's digests. I opened `report.json`, the HTML viewer/data, stdout/stderr, both JSONL files, the raw-wire JSON, and the sweep log. All 42 byte receipts reproduced their recorded byte lengths, SHA-256 values, and UTF-8 views. Authorization and set-cookie values are `[REDACTED]`; no Cerebras-key or bearer-credential pattern occurs in the extracted bundles. All nine exercised receipts passed, all three SDK retry attempts ended in injected disconnect after one real upstream chunk, and the 429 row remains explicitly `not-exercised`.32:T1a29,## Root cause

When mobile voice explicitly forces Eliza Cloud TTS (`provider === "eliza-cloud"`), the renderer still POSTed to the on-device agent's `/api/tts/cloud` proxy. On a dedicated/on-device (hybrid) runtime that proxy runs on the constrained device itself, so every reply pays an extra audio download through the phone-side Bun event loop **plus a base64 IPC re-marshal** — measured at ~5–6s/reply even though the cloud worker completes in 1–2s. The worker's provider-agnostic `POST /api/v1/voice/tts` route could serve the same `{ text }` → audio bytes directly.

Before this change, `useVoiceChat.speakElizaCloud` only bypassed the proxy for **shared-runtime** agents (`#15395`); a dedicated/on-device agent always went `resolveApiUrl("/api/tts/cloud")` → local proxy → cloud.

## The fix

`speakElizaCloud` now resolves its target through a **pure** `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute(...)` helper (`packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts`), which picks one of three targets:

1. **shared-runtime** → the worker's `/api/v1/voice/tts` off the active base (unchanged, `#15395`).
2. **direct-cloud** (`#16116`, new) → when a **cloud session bearer** (`getCloudAuthToken()` — the canonical Steward JWT) **and** a **configured cloud origin** (`configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, derived from boot config `cloudApiBase`) are both present, POST straight to `<cloudOrigin>/api/v1/voice/tts` with `Authorization: Bearer <cloud session token>`, skipping the on-device proxy entirely.
3. **on-device-proxy** → when cloud auth/config is unavailable, the existing `/api/tts/cloud` path is preserved unchanged.

The cloud origin is read from boot config (**not** a hardcoded production URL), so staging/custom cloud environments route correctly. Only the URL + bearer change — the `{ text }` body and audio-bytes response are identical.

### Acceptance criteria
- [x] Forced-cloud TTS uses the cloud session bearer token and configured cloud API origin.
- [x] Production is not hardcoded over staging/custom boot config (origin derived from `cloudApiBase`).
- [x] Existing non-forced/proxy behavior remains unchanged (fallback to `/api/tts/cloud` when no cloud auth/config; other providers untouched).
- [x] Focused headless regression proves routing and fallback.

## Files
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.ts` — `configuredCloudVoiceOrigin()`, `resolveForcedCloudTtsRoute()`, `ForcedCloudTtsRoute`.
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.ts` — `speakElizaCloud` routes via the resolver; direct path carries the cloud bearer.
- `packages/ui/src/voice/shared-runtime-voice.test.ts` — unit coverage (origin helper + resolver, all branches).
- `packages/ui/src/hooks/useVoiceChat.forced-cloud-tts.test.tsx` — hook-level regression driving the real hook/processQueue.

## How to verify
On a device running a local/on-device Eliza agent, connect Eliza Cloud (so a Steward session token exists) and set voice provider to `eliza-cloud`. Speak. The forced-cloud TTS request now hits `<cloudApiBase>/api/v1/voice/tts` directly with the cloud bearer — it no longer appears against the on-device `/api/tts/cloud` proxy (`ELIZA_TTS_DEBUG` logs `useVoiceChat:eliza-cloud-direct-worker`). Log out of Cloud (no session token) and the request falls back to `/api/tts/cloud`, unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives each selected case a typed terminal outcome: `completed`, explicit
  `capability_unavailable`, or typed `failed`.
- Captures runtime reports, failed action events, structured failure replies,
  native tool failures, trajectory aborts, and incomplete trajectories, then
  validates them fail-closed.
- Registers the production-owned knowledge graph in the real LifeOps PGLite
  test host and verifies that collaborator directly.
- Defines and tests explicit host/mobile action capability profiles.
- Uses the real, non-mutating `schedule_plan` path (`list_active`) for the hosted
  capability case instead of dispatching to an unavailable counterparty.
- Persists `report.json`, `report.md`, `cases.jsonl`, every native JSON/Markdown
  trajectory, and a SHA-256 manifest before the terminal assertion, including
  red runs.
- Uploads that bundle on every credentialed live workflow dispatch and runs the
  PR workflow against `develop`.

# Risks

Medium: this introduces the future stable merge-gate context. The aggregate is
base-trusted, read-only, hosted, bounded to 45 minutes, and never checks out PR
code. It binds every observed check to the GitHub Actions app, exact owning
workflow path, `pull_request` event, and current PR head SHA.

This PR does **not** mutate ruleset `18901247`. The existing nine no-bypass
required contexts remain authoritative throughout bootstrap and live canaries.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

Adds one always-emitted `Develop PR Gate` check for PRs targeting `develop`.
It polls the nine existing lightweight required contexts and passes only when
all nine have a current, owner-bound terminal `success`. Missing, stale,
pending past the poll budget, cancelled, timed out, failed, neutral, action
required, and skipped results fail closed. Owner workflows may classify
non-applicable work inside an always-emitted successful job; disappearing
behind a path filter is forbidden by the static workflow contract.

The workflow uses `pull_request_target` only as a trusted orchestration
boundary: it checks out `pull_request.base.sha`, has read-only permissions, has
no secrets, and observes check metadata for `pull_request.head.sha`. The new
self-test is also wired into the already-required `Test Integrity (lane
coverage)` job so this bootstrap PR validates the proposed implementation even
though a newly-added base-trusted workflow cannot execute until it exists on
the base branch.

The workflow README now distinguishes the lightweight pre-merge surface from
the exhaustive post-merge suites and the push-only `ci-ok` roll-up.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix / CI enforcement improvement.

# Documentation changes needed?

Updated `.github/workflows/README.md` with the current pre-merge and post-merge
ownership model.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

1. `packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs` — deterministic state
   transitions and trust-boundary tamper cases.
2. `.github/workflows/develop-pr-gate.yml` — stable hosted orchestration
   boundary.
3. `packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs` — exact owner,
   trigger, path-filter, permissions, and checkout contracts.

## Detailed testing steps

```text
node packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs
  develop-pr aggregate self-test passed

node packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs
  develop-pr aggregate workflow contract passed (9 contexts)

actionlint .github/workflows/develop-pr-gate.yml .github/workflows/develop-pr.yml
  exit 0

bunx @biomejs/biome check packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.mjs \
  packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate-workflow-contract.mjs \
  packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.self-test.mjs
  Checked 3 files. No fixes applied.

node packages/scripts/audit-scripts.mjs --json
  {"ok":true,"failures":[]}

git diff --check
  exit 0

bun install
  exit 0; 8859 packages installed

bun run verify
  exit 0; 573/573 uncached Turbo tasks and 28/28 dist-path consumers passed

bun test packages/scripts/develop-pr-aggregate.test.mjs --coverage
  1 pass; contract 97.54%, implementation 60.85%; enforced floor 50%
```

Deterministic terminal-state receipt at this exact head:

| Scenario | Expected aggregate | Observed |
| --- | --- | --- |
| all nine success | success | success / exit 0 |
| owner-bound context missing at deadline | failure | `missing` |
| context remains in progress at deadline | failure | `pending-timeout` |
| cancelled | failure | `terminal-cancelled` |
| timed out | failure | `terminal-timed_out` |
| failed | failure | `terminal-failure` |
| skipped/path-disappeared | failure | `skipped-forbidden` |
| wrong workflow path or wrong app | wait, then fail missing | observed |
| stale result after an event that reruns its owner | wait, then fail stale | observed |
| newer successful rerun supersedes an old failure | success | observed |

Real GitHub baseline canaries for the same nine source contexts are preserved
in #13306: [green #16321](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16321/checks)
reached 9/9 success, including the
[zero-file coverage job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298277609/job/86976439814);
[red #16322](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/pull/16322) was blocked by the
[failed title job](https://github.com/elizaOS/eliza/actions/runs/29298342833/job/86976635515).

## Post-merge aggregate canaries and migration (required before ruleset mutation)

1. Dispatch `develop-pr-gate.yml` on `develop` once for each synthetic
   `success`, `missing`, `cancelled`, `timed-out`, `failed`, `skipped`, and
   `pending-timeout` scenario. Only `success` may conclude success.
2. Open aggregate-specific green and deliberately red PR canaries. Confirm the
   aggregate observes real owner checks and that the red source lane makes the
   aggregate red.
3. Add `Develop PR Gate` to ruleset `18901247` **alongside** the existing nine
   contexts. Confirm a PR is blocked while it is missing/pending and becomes
   mergeable only after both old and new gates pass.
4. Only after those receipts, replace the nine individual required contexts
   with the aggregate. At no point is the rule open or bypassable.

Rollback is the reverse, fail-closed sequence: restore the captured nine
individual contexts alongside the aggregate first, verify effective-rule
readback and a green PR, then remove the aggregate context. If the aggregate
is unhealthy, leave the old nine in force. Never delete/disable the ruleset;
its `bypass_actors: []`, PR, deletion, and non-fast-forward rules remain
unchanged.

36:T10af,## What

Corrective follow-up to the prematurely merged #16293. This closes #16300 by making the Codex one-time refresh-token path fail closed from selection through the periodic account sweep.

- Codex selection reconciles every materialized token generation under the account refresh mutex before any canonical token resolution. Adoption failure propagates; neither `select()` nor the keep-alive sweep can fall through to `getAccessToken` with the consumed token.
- A published `auth.json` is thereafter Codex-owned. The bridge creates a refresh-token-addressed generation with atomic no-replace publication and never overwrites an existing generation, so an external Codex truncate/write cannot lose the only valid rotated pair in an adoption/write gap.
- Stable stat/read/stat retries cover the real truncate-then-write behavior of the external Codex process. Divergent generations require a real parseable `last_refresh`; ambiguous order fails instead of guessing.
- Initial materialization uses the canonical record's observable `updatedAt`, not materialization time, so it cannot invent a fresher OAuth generation.
- `config.toml` and the non-secret `active-home` pointer are atomically replaced and verified. Failure to pin `cli_auth_credentials_store = "file"` aborts the spawn.
- The benchmark adapter follows the validated active generation while preserving the pre-generation account-root layout.

## Correctness boundary

The in-process account mutex serializes reconciliation, canonical persistence, config publication, and selection. An external Codex process cannot honor that mutex, so the bridge removes the conflicting write entirely: after the initial no-replace publish, only Codex writes that generation. A later OAuth login gets a different generation path; reconciliation scans both without clobbering either.

## Verification

Exact head: `22682d9ddd4d02cb6b35e1e223ba5404eb518245`, rebased onto `origin/develop` `6e1763aed6be84a4ecb797c6cc24fe8f8ff3202b`.

- `bun run --cwd packages/app-core test --run src/services/coding-account-bridge.test.ts src/services/multi-account-refresh-failover.test.ts` — 46/46 passed. This includes a real child process that opens the shared auth file with truncation, writes half, fsyncs, pauses while two selectors race, then completes the rotation; exact external bytes survive and the canonical record adopts the rotated pair.
- Changed-file coverage gate — `account-pool.ts` 56.23%, `coding-account-bridge.ts` 89.39%, mean 72.81%; all exceed the 50% per-file threshold.

37:Td10,## What & why

The shared `pr-evidence` GitHub release hit GitHub's hard **1000-assets-per-release** cap. `gh release upload pr-evidence` now returns HTTP 422, so `scripts/pr-evidence.mjs` cannot attach media and **no surface-`.tsx` PR can satisfy the `check-pr-evidence` surface-artifact gate**. This is a repo-wide CI-infra blocker for every contributor.

## Fix

- **`scripts/pr-evidence.mjs`** — when the target release has no capacity (≥ 990-asset headroom, or a 422 `file_count` on upload), roll deterministically to the next overflow release: `pr-evidence-2`, `pr-evidence-3`, … Selection (`selectPrEvidenceTarget`) picks the highest-indexed existing release with room and creates the next one only when none has room. Dedup and URL emission span the whole family, so an asset already uploaded to any release keeps its original URL. Behavior is identical when the primary has room.
- **`scripts/check-pr-evidence.mjs`** — add `PR_EVIDENCE_RELEASE_RE` recognizing the whole `pr-evidence`/`pr-evidence-N` **download-URL** family as a first-class evidence host. Overflow-release media already validated via the media-extension check; this generalizes the non-media evidence-file recognition too. **Nothing looser**: a surface PR with no real media still fails, a link to the release *page* still fails, arbitrary hosts are not newly accepted.

Two invariants held and proven: (1) the gate stays exactly as strict; (2) existing `pr-evidence` links keep validating.

## Evidence

CI-tooling change (scripts + docs), no UI surface — visual rows are N/A. Backend-logs row points at the test/dry-run output below.

# Risks

Low. Narrows one function's error handling: a failed catalog fetch now throws instead of returning `[]`, and its single caller catches the cold-miss case. Success path and "no provider configured" (a real empty catalog) are unchanged.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

`fetchBitRouterModelCatalog` swallowed a failed OpenRouter catalog fetch and returned `[]`. `getWithSWR` caches whatever the revalidate function returns (its background revalidation writes on any non-null result, and `[] !== null`), so a **transient upstream blip overwrote the cached model catalog with an empty one for the whole TTL** — model listing/routing then sees no bitrouter models until the next successful revalidation, and the last-good stale catalog is lost.

The fix lets the fetch throw on failure (invalid shape / network error), so `getWithSWR` keeps serving the last-good stale catalog instead of caching `[]` — its background revalidation only writes on a non-throwing result. `getCachedBitRouterModelCatalog` catches the cold-miss case (no stale to serve) and degrades to `[]` rather than throwing into the hot path. "No provider configured" stays a real, cacheable empty catalog.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (resilience; non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

No.

# Testing

## Where should a reviewer start?

`packages/cloud/shared/src/lib/services/model-catalog.ts` — `fetchBitRouterModelCatalog` now throws on failure and `getCachedBitRouterModelCatalog` catches the cold miss. Pinned by `model-catalog.test.ts`.

## Detailed testing steps

None — unit tests cover it.

39:T177e,# Summary

Fixes a consistent CI failure on `develop`: the Plugin **Tests** lane (shard
2/4) times out (~90s) on
`plugins/plugin-computeruse/src/__tests__/service.integration.test.ts`.

## Root cause (corrected diagnosis)

The hang is **not** display enumeration. It is the **approval gate running
before input validation**.

`ComputerUseService.executeDesktopAction` / `executeWindowAction` call
`awaitApproval(...)` as the first step inside their `try` block, *before* any
input is validated. `awaitApproval` → `ComputerUseApprovalManager.requestApproval`
returns a Promise that only resolves when a human/API approves via the approval
UI. In the **default `smart_approve` mode**, destructive verbs (`click`, `type`,
`key`, `drag`, window `focus`, …) are **not** in `SAFE_COMMANDS`, so they are
**not** auto-approved — the Promise never resolves and the action blocks until
the 90s per-test timeout.

Why it only fails on CI: the approval mode is loaded from
`~/.eliza/computer-use-approval.json`. Developer machines that have used
computer-use have a persisted `{"mode":"full_control"}`, which auto-approves
everything, so the tests pass locally. A fresh CI runner has no such file →
default `smart_approve` → the coordinate-less `click`/`type`/`key`/`focus`
dispatches in the test hang forever.

Proven locally by pointing `HOME` at an empty dir (the exact CI condition) —
the test then times out on macOS **with a real display**, ruling out display
enumeration and isolating the approval gate as the sole cause.

## Fix

- Validate required action input **up front**, before `awaitApproval` and before
  display resolution, in both `executeDesktopAction` and `executeWindowAction`
  (`validateDesktopActionInput` / `validateWindowActionInput`). An unknown verb
  or a missing required field now **fails fast** as a typed
  `{ success:false, error }`, never entering the approval flow — no fabricated
  values, matching the repo fail-fast error policy.
- The dispatch switches keep their `require*` assertions for type narrowing /
  defense-in-depth; the new methods are the accept/reject gate. Coordinate
  requirements reuse the existing `COORDINATE_BEARING_ACTIONS` set.
- Real behavior on a host **with** a display and an approving mode is unchanged
  (valid input still validates → approves → dispatches).

## Test

- Existing tests now pass deterministically under the CI condition (empty
  `HOME` = default `smart_approve`).
- Added a regression guard that pins **deny-all `off`** mode — where the gate
  resolves *immediately* — and asserts malformed input yields the **field**
  error, not the deny message. This proves validation runs before the gate
  **without depending on a hang**, and would fail fast if the ordering
  regresses (verified: reverting the fix flips the assertion to
  `"Computer use is paused…"`). The host's approval config is saved and restored.
- The changed-file coverage gate requires ≥50% line coverage on the changed
  `computer-use-service.ts`; the prior suite only exercised validation paths
  (17.67%). `service.integration.test.ts` (mock-free, real AgentRuntime) now
  drives real, headless-safe end-to-end paths — actual filesystem I/O
  (`executeFileAction`), real child-process execution (`executeTerminalAction`),
  `executeCommand` routing across desktop/window/file/terminal, window
  read/getter/management verbs, all `normalizeEncoding` branches, and
  approval/display introspection. Browser dispatch can't be driven for real on a
  headless runner (auto-open launches a headful Chromium that hangs), so a
  dedicated `service-browser-routing.test.ts` covers it against a mocked
  `platform/browser.js` CDP boundary — exactly the pattern the package's existing
  `browser-auto-open.test.ts` uses. The changed-test gate unions both files'
  coverage of `computer-use-service.ts` to ~64% (floor 50%).

Files: plugin `.ts` + `.test.ts` only (no UI surface).

3a:T1627,## What

Three additive claude-sdk features on top of the merged effort knobs (#16313), the headline being structural Stage-1 parity for the subscription lane.

**Stage-1 ENVELOPE mode** (default on; `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_STAGE1_ENVELOPE=0` opts out). Every other Stage-1 backend structurally forces the routing envelope — anthropic native tool_use, cloud `tool_choice: required`, local GBNF. The free-text claude-sdk lane relied on prompt prose alone, so the model answered live-info asks in prose that core's tolerant parse chain accepted as a finished "simple reply" — no planner, no fetch. Live evidence: 49 of 54 live-info turns on 2026-07-13/14 were prose-shaped; every decline in that day's Discord log is this gap.

RESPONSE_HANDLER calls carrying core's `HANDLE_RESPONSE` tool now run an ENVELOPE session — one in-process MCP tool (`handle_response`), mirroring the plugin's proven ROUTE mode — whose captured envelope returns as a JSON string core parses identically to a native tool call. Design points hardened on live probes:
- The envelope **field set is derived per-turn from core's composed HANDLE_RESPONSE tool** (`params.tools`), not hardcoded — core's response-handler field REGISTRY lets any plugin add Stage-1 fields (lifeops registers `threadOps`), and the set varies by channel shape; sessions are keyed by the field set since the SDK freezes tool schemas at query() start. `HANDLE_RESPONSE_TOOL_NAME` is barrel-exported from core so the contract has one owner.
- **Shape-aware string decode:** Claude under MCP routinely stringifies array fields (`"contexts": "[\"web\"]"`); core's `Array.isArray` checks would silently default them and ship a bare ack. Fields whose declared schema type is array/object decode at the capture boundary; string-typed fields (`replyText`) pass through untouched so a legitimately JSON-shaped text answer is never mangled.
- **Evaluator isolation:** the post-tool evaluator reuses RESPONSE_HANDLER *without* the HANDLE_RESPONSE tool and expects plain JSON — it stays on the text session (routing it through envelope mode posted raw envelope JSON to the channel on a live probe).
- Off-contract prose falls back to core's tolerant text chain, so the mode strictly upgrades the lane.

**ALL-TIERS mode** (`ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_ALL_TIERS=1`): also serves the high-frequency triage tiers so the entire text brain runs on the one subscription — no weak-provider fallthrough mangling action-result rewrites. Triage uses `ELIZA_CLI_CLAUDE_SMALL_MODEL` (or the large model), never the planner tier.

**Tier metadata** (`buildModelMetadata`): `displayModel` per tier feeds RUNTIME_MODEL_CONTEXT so the agent self-reports its real serving model.

Session API: mode is fixed at construction (`text` / `route` / `envelope`), so the public surface is one `send()`.

# Risks

**Medium.** The classifier and OCR primitive are shared by every app-audit screenshot. A weak whole-page read now incurs one bounded high-contrast/sparse-text retry, which adds OCR work only below the two-word/45% reliability floor. True solid/transparent blank frames still fail from independent pixel evidence. Low-confidence nonblank frames remain visible as `needs-eyeball`; they are not promoted to healthy.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

The old rule inferred “pixels are blank” from a short OCR transcript. On the exact 390×844 issue capture (`sha256:392331bd…eff4`), whole-page packaged Tesseract saw one word, `oY)`, at 40% confidence even though 22 labeled launcher icons and the Ask Eliza bar were visibly rendered.

This change separates the signals:

- pixel analysis alone can prove blank pixels (no samples or one quantized color);
- weak OCR gets one deterministic retry: up to 3×/2400 px, normalized grayscale, sharpen, threshold 225, sparse-text segmentation;
- selected mode, raw attempt transcripts, word/character counts, and mean confidence remain in `ocr-triage.json`;
- weak OCR on nonblank pixels becomes `needs-eyeball`; OCR/image diagnostics failures remain explicit errors;
- imported OCR is enriched from the report-authorized screenshot path, never record order;
- a real packaged-Tesseract test covers a launcher capture, a genuinely solid frame, a nonblank unreadable gradient, and a near-solid warning frame.

Measured on the exact issue image, the retry recovered 49 words at 72% confidence (including Settings, Wallet, Tasks, Automations, and other launcher labels). Independent analysis measured 210 color buckets and a 33.7% dominant-color ratio, so the corrected verdict is `needs-eyeball`, `pixelBlank=false`, `regression=false`—never fabricated `verified`.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking).

# Documentation changes needed?

The OCR triage documentation is updated with the measured cause, retry configuration, confidence floor, retained diagnostics, and exact reproduction result.

# Risks

Medium. This changes top-level GitHub Actions concurrency identities for the ten workflows reused by the exhaustive develop orchestrator and for the canonical `test.yml` push signal. A malformed expression could cancel or queue the wrong work. Exact-expression mutation tests, the real manifest proof, and hosted overlap canaries guard that boundary.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

- Gives every reusable lane a caller-owned `concurrency_scope`, with `standalone` as the direct-trigger namespace and `develop-exhaustive` as the orchestrator namespace.
- Makes consecutive exhaustive runs non-cancelling and retains the full pending queue with `queue: max`.
- Keeps obsolete `test.yml` develop pushes supersedable while isolating scheduled/manual diagnostics by run ID, leaving one fail-closed aggregate result at a quiet tip.
- Extends the committed matrix proof and its mutation suite so dropped lanes, scope collisions, cancellation regressions, missing exhaustive queueing, per-run push groups, and permissive aggregate results fail closed.
- Preserves the ten-family runtime aggregate: any reusable result other than `success` remains red.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking CI orchestration correction).

# Documentation changes needed?

No public product documentation change is needed. The committed CI manifest commentary and workflow rationale document the operational contract at its source.

3e:Td90,## What

`searchMemoriesByEmbedding` was a full sequential scan computing 1536-dim cosine across the whole embeddings table on every turn. On PGlite that scan runs **WASM on the main thread**, freezing the event loop for its duration — it inflated every concurrent await (embed spans read 500–2500ms while raw provider embedding calls measure 230–320ms flat from the same box). Root-cause evidence in #16393.

Two changes:
- **`ensureEmbeddingDimension` creates the HNSW `vector_cosine_ops` index** for the ACTIVE dimension column if missing. Each row populates exactly one width column and a deployment uses one width, so fresh agents pay nothing and existing stores pay a one-time build (measured: 11k rows < 1s on PGlite/WASM). A failed build (e.g. pgvector without HNSW) warns and degrades to the previous sequential scan — search never fails closed over a missing optimization.
- **KNN-first query shape**: the inner query orders by the raw distance operator over the embeddings table alone — the only shape the index can serve; the previous `similarity >= threshold` + join form forced the planner into the scan. Filters and the threshold apply to a bounded candidate pool (overfetch `clamp(count*16, 256, 1024)`), keeping worst-case work flat regardless of table size. Works identically on the real-Postgres adapter (shared base).

## Evidence

| Type | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Tests | Real-PGlite integration suite (no mocks): index exists after `ensureEmbeddingDimension`; ranking order across known-cosine vectors; threshold cuts the orthogonal vector; type filtering applies through the KNN pool; the pre-existing similarity search test still passes. 31/31 in `memory.real.test.ts`, 90/90 unit lane. |
| Domain artifacts | Live deployment: offline build on the production store — `rows with dim_1536: 11165`, `index built <1s`, `pg_indexes` shows `idx_embeddings_dim_1536_hnsw_cosine`; post-restart live probes fetch + answer correctly (BTC price turn end-to-end). |
| Backend logs | InferenceTiming before/after on the same box (with the sibling context fixes): simple-chat time-to-reply 5.9s median → 1.4–1.8s. |

3f:T2654,Closes #16187

## Problem

Root `test:cloud` invoked `packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs` with no build prerequisite, so after a deterministic clean install (`ELIZA_SKIP_ARTIFACT_SYNC=1 bun install --frozen-lockfile`) the batches started against a tree with no `@elizaos/core` dist and no generated i18n keyword modules. Reproduced on this exact worktree at the merge-base (`ab1f1bd870c`), unmodified runner: **exit 1, 10 of 11 batches red, 669 failed tests + 424 errors**, all module-resolution cascades that read like database regressions.

The actual failure chain has three shapes (counts from the before log):

| Shape | Cause | Occurrences |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `Cannot find module '@elizaos/core' from plugins/plugin-sql/src/index.ts` | `@elizaos/core` resolves via its `bun` export condition to `packages/core/dist/node/index.node.js` — absent before a build | 142 |
| `Cannot find module './generated/validation-keyword-data.ts' from packages/core/src/i18n/…` | generated keyword module missing (core's prebuild emits it only on a real, uncached build) | 349 |
| `Cannot find module './generated/validation-keyword-data.js' from packages/shared/src/i18n/keyword-matching.ts` | generated keyword module missing in `@elizaos/shared` | 1 |

## Fix

A clean-install preflight inside `test-cloud-run.mjs` (`ensureCloudTestRuntime`), run by `main()` before any batch:

- checks the exact artifacts the cloud lane's import graph resolves: the three generated keyword modules and `packages/core/dist/node/index.node.js`;
- if keyword modules are missing → runs `packages/shared/scripts/generate-keywords.mjs` (the same script core's prebuild and shared's `build:i18n` use);
- if the core dist is missing → runs `packages/scripts/build-core.mjs` (the root `build:core` — the exact prerequisite `test:server`/`test:client`/`test:plugins` and the cloud-tests workflow's `cloud-setup-test-env` action already use: its `build:linked-workspaces` is literally `bun run --cwd ../../.. build:core`);
- re-checks after each step and **fails loudly** (step name + exit code/signal, no batches started) if a step fails or completes without producing its artifacts.

Placing the preflight in the runner (instead of a `build:core && …` prefix in package.json) keeps `windows-ci.yml`'s direct `node packages/scripts/test-cloud-run.mjs` invocation covered, costs a fully built tree only four `existsSync` calls, and repairs the turbo-cache-hit shape: the `build` task's `outputs` cache `dist/**` only, so a cache hit can restore core's dist without ever running the keyword codegen — a bare `build:core &&` prerequisite would not fix that state. The keyword-generation step is now performed by the command itself rather than being tribal knowledge (issue acceptance criterion).

PGlite readiness assertions and the runner's existing fail-closed guards are untouched.

## Tests

- **`packages/scripts/test-cloud-run-preflight.self-test.mjs`** (new; wired as `test:cloud:preflight:self-test` and as a step in `cloud-tests.yml` before `bun run test:cloud`): executes the preflight logic against simulated states — clean install (asserts codegen runs before build:core and each group is satisfied), turbo-cache-hit-without-codegen (asserts only codegen runs), dist-only-missing, fully built (asserts zero side effects); asserts the loud-failure contract (step exit code / signal / spawn error / step-succeeded-but-artifacts-still-missing / artifact group without a step); and **runs the real keyword codegen** against the repo to prove the preflight's artifact list matches what the script actually emits (path-drift guard, not a re-read of the wiring string).
- **`packages/scripts/__tests__/test-cloud-run.test.mjs`**: 9 new bun:test cases covering `ensureCloudTestRuntime` and `runPreflightStep` (25 pass total in the file).

# Risks

Medium. These scripts assemble publish output for many workspaces. The main risks are exposing a duplicate canonical package identity that traversal order previously hid, or excluding generated Python state a package accidentally depended on. Representative agent, app-core, and UI outputs were therefore built, packed, and inspected directly; ordinary Python sources remain present.

# Background

## What does this PR do?

It prevents local non-canonical state from contaminating package output:

- `prepare-package-dist` now resolves dependency versions through the repository's shared canonical workspace-discovery seam. Nested tool manifests cannot impersonate a workspace root, workspace negations remain authoritative, malformed selected manifests fail, and duplicate canonical names are rejected.
- `copy-package-assets` excludes Python bytecode, cache/type/lint/test state, virtual environments, coverage residue, and `*.egg-info` metadata while retaining source assets.

The CLI boundary still emits a non-zero build failure rather than a partial manifest. No package manifest, lockfile, workflow, runtime, UI, or generated packaging-runtime exclusion changed.

## What kind of change is this?

Bug fix (non-breaking build and publishing hardening).

# Documentation changes needed?

N/A - no user-facing command or configuration contract changed; the behavior is internal package assembly and is covered by executable tests.

## Known gaps / failures

N/A - all applicable post-sync gates passed. Cached `bun-ios-runtime` warnings and report-only test-realness findings are unrelated to this four-path diff.41:T1546,## What

Live-info turns ("whats btc at right now", weather asks) were misrouting or burning planner budget through four structural gaps. Root-caused from live Discord trajectories on 2026-07-14; each fix is structural (no phrase lists, no per-asset regexes):

1. **Directions are not layout operations.** `LEFT/RIGHT/TOP/BOTTOM` sat in the view heuristics' layout operation group, so the temporal adverb "right now" (RIGHT = layout op + NOW = layout follow-up) turned any live-info ask into a VIEWS candidate that narrowed WEB_FETCH off the planner surface. Directions now only count as operation evidence alongside an explicit surface noun or a concrete capability-token match — "arrange the windows right now" and "move it to the left of the screen" still route to views; "whats btc at right now" cannot.
2. **Stage 1 routes live-info to planning instead of declining.** The `messageHandlerTemplate` rule told the model to "decline plainly" when "no tool is available to fetch it this turn" — but Stage 1 cannot see the tool surface; the planner can. The rule now routes current/live asks to planning (non-simple contexts, requiresTool, web/fetch hint, brief ack) and moves the plain decline to the planner reply. The personal-crisis deferral explicitly takes precedence so a crisis ask with a live-info component (ER hours) never gates life-safety guidance behind a fetch.
3. **The named-tool gate only hard-enforces resolvable candidates.** A Stage-1 hint that resolves to no tool exposed to the planner this turn (e.g. a web hint on a runtime with no web action) cannot be satisfied — enforcing it burned the full required-tool miss budget re-rejecting the planner's honest answer. Candidates resolve through the runtime action lookup (names + similes), so a simile-named candidate (`SPAWN_AGENT` for `TASKS`) keeps enforcement.
4. **Heuristic-evidence early accept.** When the tool requirement stands on deterministic text inference alone (`requiredToolEvidence: "inferred"` — the model named no candidate), a planner that re-commits the IDENTICAL terminal answer on consecutive misses is accepted early, mirroring the widget-identity escape (#15230). Observed live: 4 identical REPLYs / ~36s for an opinion ask force-planned by an inferred web candidate; now 2 calls. Model-named requirements keep the full corrective budget.

Also: the context-search web keyword doc gains the live-info value terms (price/worth/how much/weather/forecast/temperature/exchange rate) so keyword retrieval scores WEB_FETCH on natural phrasings.

42:T189f,Closes #16180

## Problem

`packages/tools/voice-evidence-harness/src/cli.ts` derived both the evidence root and the Deepgram/Cartesia secret paths from `node:os.homedir()` inline, and `cli-run.test.ts` only reassigned `process.env.HOME` — which Bun's `homedir()` ignores — so every test run wrote evidence under the **real** `~/.moltbot` tree. Reproduced on this machine before the fix: running the develop-side `cli-run.test.ts` leaked `~/.moltbot/projects/eliza-fleet/evidence/voice-e2e/2026-07-13T20-11-15-312Z/` (108-byte fake `input.wav`, 4-byte fake `walkthrough.mp4`) into the real home (see linked evidence). The leaked dirs were removed after capture, mirroring the issue's remediation.

## Change

- **`src/paths.ts` (new)** — single injectable derivation seam. `VOICE_EVIDENCE_ROOT` / `VOICE_EVIDENCE_SECRETS_DIR` env overrides win; a set-but-blank or relative override **throws** (a malformed override must never silently fall back to the real home); defaults preserve the exact live `~/.moltbot` layout and evidence naming. Runtime-neutral by design (only `node:os`/`node:path`, erasable TS) so the same file executes under Bun and Node type stripping.
- **`src/cli.ts`** — evidence root + secret paths now come from the seam; no other behavior change.
- **`src/cli-run.test.ts`** — rewritten to run against the **real filesystem** (the `node:fs` `mock.module` is gone): real committed fixture WAV, real secret JSONs read from the temp secrets dir through the seam. Asserts on the **pass path** (in-process baseline run) that the full DoD artifact set lands below the `mkdtemp` root, and on the **failure path** (a spawned, fully **unmocked** `bun src/cli.ts` subprocess failing loudly on a missing `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`) that the partial evidence tree also stays below the override root. Every test snapshots the real `~/.moltbot` tree (names, kinds, sizes, mtimes) before, and asserts **zero delta** after — in the test bodies and again in `afterEach` as a safety net.
- **`src/paths.test.ts` (new)** — derivation contract under **both runtimes**: Bun leg via direct imports (override wins without consulting the home lookup, defaults on the `~/.moltbot` layout incl. the live `homedir()` default, blank/relative overrides throw), plus a **Node** leg that runs the identical assertions in a type-stripped `node` subprocess and compares observations, so a Bun-vs-Node divergence (the #16180 failure mode) surfaces as a concrete value diff.
- **`README.md`** — documents the two overrides.

## Acceptance criteria mapping

| criterion | where |
| --- | --- |
| explicit injectable evidence root + test-safe secret seam | `src/paths.ts` (`VOICE_EVIDENCE_ROOT`, `VOICE_EVIDENCE_SECRETS_DIR`, injectable env/home params) |
| preserve current live default under the real user home | `DEFAULT_EVIDENCE_SUBPATH` / `DEFAULT_SECRETS_SUBPATH`; asserted in `paths.test.ts` ("default stays on the ~/.moltbot layout", incl. real `homedir()` leg) |
| every artifact below the test mkdtemp root on success and failure | `cli-run.test.ts` — pass path walks the produced tree and checks the full DoD artifact set + `startsWith(tmpRoot)`; failure path via real unmocked CLI subprocess |
| real `~/.moltbot` has no delta after the test | `snapshotTree()` before/after (names, kinds, sizes, mtimes) in both tests + `afterEach` safety net |
| exercised under both Node and Bun | `paths.test.ts` Node subprocess leg (type-stripped import of the same module) + all Bun legs; verified on node v25.2.1 locally, CI runs node 24.15 (type stripping default since 23.6) |
| live-provider behavior and evidence naming unchanged outside tests | overrides only read from env; defaults byte-identical; run-dir naming (`<stamp>[-real-server]/<scenario>`) untouched |

🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)43:T143f,## Motivation

The desktop UI dossier identified three linked causes behind Settings feeling like a stretched phone UI:

1. Desktop mode changed mechanics, not composition. Settings remained one wide vertical column.
2. Opening a section replaced navigation instead of preserving place awareness.
3. The grouped mobile list was serving as the desktop information architecture instead of a touch-specific presentation.

This PR implements the dossier's independent P0.1 slice only. Desktop Settings now has a persistent grouped navigation rail and real work area. The existing mobile hub and drill-in flow remains unchanged below 1024px.

## What changed

- Added a 240px grouped desktop settings rail sourced from `settings-sections.ts`.
- Kept the active section highlighted while switching content in place.
- Added Arrow Up/Down focus movement, Enter activation, persistent visible focus treatment, 44px targets, and the launcher back affordance.
- Added a desktop content work area with 24px/32px responsive padding and a localized breadcrumb/title.
- Preserved the current mobile grouped hub, section drill-in, loading/error boundaries, hashes, and agent-surface anchors.
- Added focused Vitest coverage for rail rendering, active state, breakpoint behavior, and keyboard navigation.

44:Tc74,## What

`PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` cost 328–3394ms on the Stage-1 critical path every turn — doing work whose output is **stripped before the prompt** (`omitRecentMessages`; the transcript is owned by RECENT_MESSAGES, and the fetched history has zero runtime consumers — grepped packages/ + plugins/). Three deterministic fixes, no deadline, no dropped context (the deadline alternative is #16400; root-cause evidence in #16393):

- **plugin-discord `getConnectorChatContext`** reads recent history from the gateway-populated message cache instead of a REST `GET /channels/{id}/messages` that queues behind discord.js's per-route rate-limit buckets (100ms–3s+ per call). Same gateway-delivered data, zero cost; empty only right after boot. The room DB read now only runs when the target carries no channelId.
- **`platformContext` drops shadowed legacy connectors**: connector plugins register a legacy accountId-less entry plus one per account; the pair answers every hook with byte-identical work, so each turn did the full fetch **twice, serially**.
- **Both connector hook loops run concurrently** — each hook may do its own I/O and this provider sits on the compose critical path; serializing stacks every connector's round-trip into the turn floor. Protects every other connector platform implementing these hooks, not just Discord.

## Evidence

| Type | Evidence |
|---|---|
| Real-LLM trajectory | Live before/after on the same deployment (InferenceTiming spans): `provider:PLATFORM_CHAT_CONTEXT` 328–3394ms → **145–235ms**. Live probes post-change: chat + price turns answer correctly with real fetched data (coingecko ETH $1,927.46), one clean message each. |
| Backend logs | span table in #16393 (comment) — before rows show chatctx up to 3394ms; post-restart rows 145/235ms. |
| Tests | plugin-discord suite 433/433 (the account-pool connector test now exercises the gateway-cache contract); core platformContext suite green. |
| Screenshots / video / frontend logs | N/A - provider/runtime change, no UI surface. |
| Domain artifacts | N/A - the InferenceTiming span deltas above are the domain artifact. |
